## Consequential corrections

- 149_scan130, Right marginal note, opening: `C'est visiblement \uline{faux}` / `C'est \uncertain{sûrement} « \uncertain{en général} » \emph{faux}` → `C'est visiblement \uline{faux}`. The word after C'est begins v with a dot over the following i; two tall stems belong to b and l, followed by a compressed -ement ending. The separate faux has a large looped f and terminal crossed x and an underline.
- 149_scan130, Right marginal note, question about minimal model: `Mais y a-t-il un modèle « minimal » $X\supset U$` / `\textbf{Mais} \uncertain{est-il vrai} \uncertain{qu'on} \ill{} $X\supset U$` → `Mais y a-t-il un modèle &#96;&#96;minimal'' $X\supset U$`. After Mais a descending y and the short a-t-il sequence precede un. modèle has the same looped d and high l seen above. The following word has the low repeated minims of minimal and a high terminal l, bounded by pairs of quote ticks. X, the open superset sign and U stand on the next marginal baseline.
- 149_scan130, Right marginal note, end of question: `et pour un tel modèle \unsure{a-t-on} l'injectivité ???` / `\uncertain{tel que} \ill{} \uncertain{localement} \emph{injectif}…` → `et pour un tel modèle \unsure{a-t-on} l'injectivité ???`. The note contains a separate et with a crossed t, then pour with a p descender, un and tel modèle. The short following group is compressed at the edge and a-t-on remains doubtful. In the final word the j descends, the -tivité ending continues beyond the f-shaped middle stroke, and terminal question hooks/dots replace an ellipsis.
- 149_scan130, L12, parameter-space symbol: `$\unsure{\mathfrak X_{L/K}|\mathcal Z_{L/K}}$` / `$\mathfrak{X}_{L/K}$` → `$\mathfrak X_{L/K}$`. The main glyph has two upper arms meeting two lower curved arms at a central crossing, with a short transverse bar. It is X-shaped, with no Z-like top-to-bottom diagonal. The subscript is L / K. The whole short formula was read on the crop; this resolves C's alternative.
- 149_scan130, L13, first cancelled predicate: `\deleted{est injectif}` / `\struck{est égal}` → `\deleted{est \unsure{injectif}}`. The crossed lower word has the long j descender and terminal t/i/f-like strokes, but its initial/medial joins are obscured, so injectif remains a candidate rather than a certain word. Above it the visible insertion starts une; the area before that word is blank and supplies no independent a.
- 149_scan130, L18, stacked cancelled and replacement predicates: `\deleted{est injective} \deleted{\unsure{surjective}} \inserted{a une image ouverte}` / `\struck{soit \uncertain{à image} \ill{} \uncertain{ouverte}}` → `\deleted{est \unsure{injective}} \deleted{\inserted{\unsure{surjective}}} \inserted{a une image ouverte}`. The lower old predicate has a long j descender but its middle letters merge with the strike and other insertions. The upper surjective candidate is itself crossed; a une image ouverte remains live. Reversing the nested deleted/inserted wrappers does not change that state.
- 149_scan130, L18–19, later dark-ink conclusion: `\inserted{--- sans doute est-elle $=$ surjective.}` / `\add{\uncertain{soit}} surjective.` → `\inserted{--- sans doute est-elle $=$ surjective.}`. Above the cancelled pencil sentence the darker ink reads sans (initial two-lobed s), doute (looped d and crossed t), est-elle (two high l stems) and two horizontal equals strokes. The continuation surjective. is dark and live on the following baseline, also visible in crop P17. The black horizontal line below the first part cancels the older pencil sentence, not the new dark words.
- 149_scan132, L10 after justement: `justement, à sous-groupes` / `justement, à \uncertain{conjugaison} — et \uncertain{à} \uncertain{sous-groupes}` → `justement, à sous-groupes`. After the comma there is one accented à followed immediately by the short sous- group at the right end of this line. No intervening conjugaison, second dash, et, or second à is present.
- 149_scan132, L12 corrected relation after V: `V\deleted{\unsure{\to}}\inserted{\in}\mathfrak X_{L/K}` / `V \in \mathfrak{X}_{L/K} \struck{\ill{}}` → `V\deleted{\unsure{\to}}\inserted{\in}\mathfrak X_{L/K}`. The live membership sign is above a cancelled horizontal relation between V and the calligraphic X. A bent right-end stroke supports the tentative arrow reading, but the crossing-out prevents clearing C’s doubt. The deleted ink is in this gap, not after the L/K subscript as H places its illegible deletion.
- 149_scan132, L16 superscript on D_J: `D_J^{\unsure{\circ}}` / `D_J^{*}` → `D_J^*`. The raised mark has several crossing dark strokes and projecting tips around a filled centre; it is not a hollow circular superscript. The whole display was inspected with this mark. C’s doubt is resolved to an asterisk.
- 149_scan132, L16 brace label and right-hand closed intersection: `\overbrace{\bigcap_{j\in J}D_j}^{\Delta_J}-\bigcup_{i\in I-J}\Delta_J\cap D_i` / `\overbrace{\bigcap_{j \in J} D_j}^{D_J} - \bigcup_{i \in I \setminus J} D_J \cap D_i` → `\overbrace{\bigcap_{j\in J}D_j}^{\Delta_J}-\bigcup_{i\in I\unsure{\setminus|-}J}\Delta_J\cap D_i`. Both occurrences are closed triangular signs with a diagonal left side, diagonal right side and horizontal base, followed by J. The D_j under the brace instead has an upright stem and rounded bowl. Thus both closed-intersection labels are Delta_J, not D_J.
- 149_scan132, L17 cardinality variable: `d=\operatorname{card}J` / `\alpha = \operatorname{card} J` → `d=\operatorname{card}J`. The variable has a small rounded bowl and a separate high ascender on its right, the same d form as the subsequent codimension variable; an alpha has no such tall d stem here.
- 149_scan132, L18 de codim d: `de codim $d$` / `de codim $\alpha$` → `de codim\textsuperscript{\unsure{n}} $d$`. The variable after codim again has a round low bowl with a tall upright ascender, matching the d in d=card J; the ascender is the distinguishing stroke.
- 149_scan132, Margin underlined sous-schéma: `\uline{sous-schéma}` / `\uncertain{l'ensemble}` → `\uline{\unsure{sous-schém.|sous-schéma}}`. The underlined group begins sous-, followed by the high sch and accented e. Its final minims end before an independent dot. A short schém. ending is preferred, but the cramped last joins still admit schéma; neither reading is l’ensemble. See independent crop B22.
- 149_scan132, Margin définir son support / sous-ouvert: `définir son \uline{\unsure{support|sous-ouvert}}` / `\uncertain{décrire}` → `définir son \uline{support}`. The word has the initial s/u run, two deep p descenders, low o/r joins and final t. There is no separating hyphen or additional ouvert sequence. The independent crop settles C’s underlined noun to support; son and the noun are absent from H.
- 149_scan132, Margin final set relation: `I(x)\unsure{=|\supseteq}J` / `I(x) \supset J` → `I(x)=J`. Between the closing parenthesis and J there are two separate heavy horizontal bars of equal orientation. There is no right-facing curved or angular enclosure at their right ends. The complete marginal set formula was read; the equality is preserved despite the possible mathematical mismatch with a closed intersection.
- 149_scan132, L17 last term inside the union: `\unsure{D_i\cap D_j}` / `D_i\cap D_j` → `\unsure{D_i\cap D|D_i\cap D_j}`. The first D has braced i; the right-hand subtraction slash, enclosing parenthesis, union and j not-equal i bound are visible. The compressed interior has D_i and a cap-like join followed by a last D. No separate final j can be isolated between that D and the closing parenthesis, although the crowded terminal curve could hide it. The point is independent.
- 149_scan132, Margin inclusion sign immediately before the intersection: `\bigcap_{j\in J}D_j` / `\bigcap_{i\in J}D_i` → `\subset\bigcap_{j\in J}D_j`. The first symbol has a rounded D bowl, not the triangular Delta of the main display. The low joins after it support one comme; no separately spaced un is secure. The underlined sous-schém group ends in compressed minims and an independent dot, so the abbreviated ending is preferred but remains uncertain. A separate open inclusion curve stands before the intersection. The number (36) is upright in the original and becomes tilted when the oblique writing is levelled; it aligns with the main display and is not part of the marginal sentence. After the marginal formula a separate comma precedes et. In the later underlined word, the two deep p descenders and terminal t support support, without a hyphen or the longer ouvert sequence. The final relation has two heavy parallel bars and no enclosing curve, so equality remains the reading.
- 149_scan133, Top interlinear parenthesis, whole formula: `\inserted{(i.e. $\unsure{\underline{D}_J|\bar D_J}=\bigcap_{j\in J}D_j$ \unsure{soit} $\ne\varnothing$)}` / `\add{(i.e.\ $D_J = \bigcap_{j \in J} D_j$ soit $\neq \emptyset$)},` → `\inserted{(i.e. $\bar D_J=\bigcap_{j\in J}D_j$ soit $\ne\phi$)}`. A separate dark horizontal stroke lies above D; the hook below is J, not an underline. The small word has s, closed o, an i minim and a final t upright. The final empty-set sign again has a loop and long traversing stem. No separate comma follows the closing parenthesis. This resolves C marks M0183 and M0184 from the crop of the complete insertion.
- 149_scan133, L2, first replaced noun and adjective: `\deleted{\unsure{solide}} \inserted{maquette} simpliciale` / `\struck{schéma} \add{\uncertain{ensemble}} simplicial` → `\deleted{\unsure{solide}} \inserted{maquette} \unsure{,} simpliciale`. - 149_scan133, L4, correspondent comp. connexes: `correspondent aux comp. connexes` / `correspondent \supplied{aux} comp.\ connexes` → `correspondent comp. connexes`. - 149_scan133, L5, ou encore : celles de: `ou encore : celles de` / `on connaît : celles du \uncertain{bout} de` → `ou encore : celles de`. The first word ends with the open u return, not n. Encore has the low e/n/c sequence, round o and terminal re, without the ascenders/accents needed for connaît. After celles the ink is the short de; no independent du bout sequence intervenes.
- 149_scan133, L6 interlinear addition before complété: `le \inserted{\unsure{l'ombre}} complété` / `le \add{\uncertain{formel}} complété` → `le \inserted{l'ombre} complété`. - 149_scan133, L7, a un caractère invariant: `a un caractère invariant p.r.` / `\ill{} une \uncertain{notion} \uncertain{invariante} \uncertain{p.r.}` → `a un caractère invariant p.r.`. - 149_scan133, Lower left oblique margin note: `\margin{il faut choisir $\tilde D_J$ &#96;&#96;au dessus de $\bar K$''\ldots}` / `\marginal{\ill{} \uncertain{dessus} $\widetilde{D}_J$ \ill{} $U_J$ \ill{} \uncertain{revêtement universel} \ill{} $D_J$ \ill{}}` → `\margin{il faut choisir $\tilde D_J$ &#96;&#96;au dessus de $\bar K$''\ldots}`. - 149_scan133, Small marginal annotation by the first term of (37): `\margin{plutôt $T^J$}` / `` → `\margin{\unsure{plutot|plutôt} \unsure{ou|en} ci-contre)}`. - 149_scan133, NB margin, second U formula: `$\unsure{U_{J\alpha}}$` / `$U_J$` → `$U_{J\alpha}$`. - 149_scan133, NB margin, tilde X formula: `$\unsure{\tilde X_{J\alpha}}$` / `` → `$\tilde X_{J\alpha}$`. After d’où the formula has two crossing diagonals for X, a separate wavy tilde above, a hooked J below/right and a small looped alpha. - 149_scan133, NB margin, heading and beginning: `\uline{NB} Il faut partir de` / `` → `\uline{NB} \unsure{Il|$\tilde U_J$} \unsure{faut partir|fait partie} de`. NB and its underline are clear. The joined initial body and low hook permit Il or a decorated U_J, and the following compressed words permit faut partir or fait partie. The first reading is retained as preferred but its earlier certainty is withdrawn. H still omits the block.
- 149_scan133, NB margin, continuation omitted or fragmented in H: `$\unsure{U_{J\bar K}}$, \unsure{si on veut}, d'un rev. universel $\unsure{\tilde V_0}$ de $\unsure{U_{J\alpha}}$ (qu'on va \unsure{multiplier} au dessus de $\bar K$ bien sûr), d'où $\unsure{\tilde X_{J\alpha}}$, $\unsure{\tilde D_{J\alpha}}$.` / `\marginal{\ill{} \uncertain{dessus} $\widetilde{D}_J$ \ill{} $U_J$ \ill{} \uncertain{revêtement universel} \ill{} $D_J$ \ill{}}` → `$U_J$, \unsure{i.e.}, d'un rev. universel $\unsure{C_0|\tilde V_0}$ de $U_{J\alpha}$ (qu'on va \unsure{multiplier} au dessus de $\bar K$ bien sûr), d'où $\tilde X_{J\alpha}$, $\unsure{\tilde D_{J\alpha}}$.`. This broad rotated crop includes neighboring notes and main-text strokes. In the NB block the opening resembles Il faut partir but the joined initial body and low hook also admit the independent reader's tilde-U_J fait partie; it cannot remain wholly certain. After de the first U has a clear hooked J; the supposed barred K and si on veut are not recoverable as separate letters from the crossing façon/précise strokes. The surviving small short-form cluster is compatible with i.e. and remains doubtful. The small cover-symbol body has a rounded open C-like curve and low oval rather than a clear V point; no independent tilde is secure there. The later U_Jalpha, qu'on va, the uncertain multiplier, au dessus de barred K, bien sûr, d'où, and the tilded X/D clusters can be followed, with their previous local doubts retained. The foliation, equation tag and other two notes in this wide box are not duplicated.
- 149_scan134, L7 identification below pi_1(fibre): `\underset{\substack{\Vert\\ T^J}}{\pi_1(\text{fibre})}` / `\note{sous « $\pi_1(\text{fibre})$ » est écrit « $= T^J$ ».}` → `\underset{\substack{\Vert\\ T^J}}{\pi_1(\text{fibre})}`.
